### 2026-06-30 — Grid viewer: brain-map y-scale was clipping responses (NOT a data bug); fixed to ±5%
**Changed/Found:** User reported the brain maps "weren't showing the trial-averaged response, something else instead." ROOT CAUSE diagnosed: NOT a plotting/indexing bug — proved `interactive_grid.py` brain-map blue == the trials-window orange mean to 9e-9 (same H[s,r]). The real problem was the FIXED Y-SCALE: it was set from the 99.5th percentile of *per-sample* |H| (~±1.9%), which is dominated by the many near-zero samples and CLIPPED 13.7% of pairs at their peak — real responses reach 4-7% (e.g. the (0.5,0)→(0.5,-1) pair dips to -4.5%), so the transient was sliced off and only the small pre-stim neighbour-ripples remained visible. Fixed: `YMAX = 0.05` (±5% dF/F), which clips only ~0.1% of pairs; added `--ymax` override. Diagnostic saved to grid_png/diag_scale.png (same curve at two scales). User chose fixed ±5% (comparable across all panels) over per-node/per-map autoscale.
**Why:** Per-sample percentile is the wrong basis for a response-amplitude scale; per-pair peaks span 0.9% (median) to 7% (max), so ±1.9% clipped the strong/interesting responses.
**Next:** none (scale resolved). Phase 2 network model still pending.

### 2026-06-30 — Grid viewer simplified to single-click 2-map design + mean made prominent
**Changed/Found:** `interactive_grid.py` — (1) Simplified per user: one click X on the PRIMARY map now drives BOTH the efferent map (H[X,·]) and the AFFERENT map (column H[·,X]: each site Y = X's response when Y was stimmed); clicking a node Y on the afferent map opens the 50-trial grid for (stim Y → readout X). Removed the "Inspect Y" button/mode. (2) Fixed the user-reported "brain maps look like single trials": the thin blue mean was the same colour as and buried under the huge ±2 SD band — made the mean a thick (lw 1.7) solid blue line over a faint (alpha 0.10) band, so the trial average is unmistakable. Confirmed the brain-map blue IS H (= the trials-window orange mean) to 1e-8. (3) Fixed a default-pair bug: preview Y0 was landing on X0 (self), collapsing the red+green frames into one — now forced to the strongest NON-self afferent driver.
**Why:** User wanted a simpler two-map flow keyed off one click, and noticed the averaged response wasn't reading as an average on the brain panels.
**Next:** Residual blue wiggle past ~±0.7 s is real neighbour-stim contamination (ITI ~0.71 s), already handled by the ≤1 s TF-fit cap — leave as honest display. Phase 2 network model still pending.

### 2026-06-29 — [CP-HEMI]: causal DC re-anchoring (cp_anchor_pred) fixes the per-segment level shift
**Changed/Found:** The whole-hemi readout has no intercept + z-scores contra regressors over the whole block, so the prediction's absolute DC level drifts per segment → raw per-segment R² goes negative on the WORST segments even when the shape is right (the level-offset cases). New helper `utils/cp_anchor_pred.m`: `yhat_a = yhat + mean(yact(win) − yhat(win))` — a single causal DC shift anchored on a stim-free window (lead-in for spontaneous, PRE-STIM baseline for stim trials). Wired into `contra_prediction.m` `[CP-HEMI]`: added knob `h_anchor_s=1.0`s, a third per-segment metric `h_segR2a` (DC-anchored) + pooled `h_poolA`, and both the examples + best/worst figures now plot raw pred (dashed) vs DC-anchored pred (bold) with `raw|anch|shape` R² in the titles (replaces last turn's centred-dashed pair). Quantified on AL_0033 held-out segments (n=190): median per-seg R² **raw 0.798 → anch(1.0s) 0.878 → shape 0.894** (ceiling); negatives **8% → 3%**; the 3 worst segments **−2.61/−2.04/−1.50 → +0.71/+0.56/+0.51** (= their shape R²). 0.5s lead too short (noisy DC, min −4.16); 1.0s stable. check_matlab_code clean (helper 0 issues).
**Why:** User: the WORST-case row doesn't account for the shift; fix it now so the level offset doesn't contaminate the stim residual when we move to stim prediction. The DC offset, if left in, injects a spurious baseline into residual = actual − pred (the local stim effect). `cp_anchor_pred` is the reusable mechanism: anchoring on the pre-stim baseline makes the stim residual the pure local response.
**Next:** When building trial-level stim prediction (red = contra pred + TF; residual over the 0–200 ms dip), call `cp_anchor_pred(yact, yhat, preStimWin)` so each trial's prediction is DC-matched to its pre-stim baseline before differencing — replaces relying on post-hoc per-trial baseline subtraction. Optional: a DC+slope anchor (linear detrend on the lead-in) if within-segment drift remains. Commit with the other cp_* deliverables.

### 2026-06-29 — Found: near-midline sign mixing + AIC over-selects TF order
**Changed/Found:** From the cached tensor: only 11/26 left (excitatory) sites have a positive diagonal peak — the x=−0.5 column is almost all negative, i.e. ROIs within ~0.5 mm of midline are dominated by the opposite hemisphere's (inhibitory) field; clearly-lateral sites (|x|≥1.5) are correctly signed. Separately, AIC always selects the highest order offered (3, then 4) — smooth/autocorrelated residual makes added poles always lower SSE, so naïve AIC over-fits order.
**Why:** Both affect the full-matrix TF program: the readout-definition near midline, and the model-order criterion.
**Next:** (1) decide readout handling near midline (accept ROI-mean + let the network model's cross-coupling explain it / focal-demix / restrict to lateral); (2) switch order selection to BIC or trial-split cross-validation (~48 trials/site) — recommend BIC for the 2704-pair scan + CV spot-check.

### 2026-06-29 — Data-derived stim-site localizer: AL_0033 laser spot = native (373,353); user adopts it as canonical
**Changed/Found:** Built `utils/cp_find_stim_site.m` — localises the photostim site purely from data (independent of the flip-prone `params.pixel`): trial-averages the peri-stim widefield response in SVD space (baseline −500→0 ms, peak 0→200 ms, strongest amp), reconstructs the full native frame (`svdFrameReconstruct`, same orientation as `pixelTuningCurveViewerSVD`/`loadUVt`), and takes the deepest %ΔF/F (most-inhibited) pixel. Ran on AL_0033 0129 (loaded U/V/mimg from server, nSV=200; stim onsets from cached `stim_vars`): **laser site = native [row 373, col 353]**, robust (strongest-amp and pooled top-3 both give 373,353; deep-0.5% centroid ≈365,353). The photoinhibition is one clean focal blob centred there. Of the two `params.pixel=[380,320]` reads: **B=transpose (row380,col320)** sits inside the blob; **A=getpixel read (row320,col380)** sits on the blob RIM ~60 px off — so the `getpixel_dFoF` read (`mimg(pixel(2),pixel(1))`) is the flipped one. Kernel-mean impulse traces at A vs the data site are ~95% identical (dip −3.65 vs −3.94), so the recorded trace was on the inhibition edge but still captured the response. Saved canonical site → `data/cp_stim_site_AL_0033_0129_e1.mat`. **USER DECISION: adopt the data-derived site (373,353) as canonical** (knowing it retargets y_full from the rim to the true centre).
**Why:** User: be certain of the laser spot from the data (params.pixel keeps x/y-flipping via load/save), use the kernel viewer as verifier. The most-inhibited trial-averaged pixel is the unambiguous, orientation-free ground truth.
**Next:** Wire the canonical site into `contra_prediction.m` (new `[CP-SITE]` section: load `cp_stim_site` cache → set `px_prim=row, py_prim=col`, re-extract `y_full` at it; `[CP-HEMI]` footprint + residual sections then target the true site), and fix the static `[CP-KERNEL]`/`[CP-BLEED]` markers to the consistent native-on-transposed rule (`plot(px_prim,py_prim)`). Verify by re-running `[CP-SETUP]`→`[CP-HEMI]` (expect R² ≈ unchanged). Replicate localizer on AL_0041 e1/e2.

### 2026-06-29 — Diagnosed the contra-kernel x/y flip: transposed display + native-frame marker code
**Changed/Found:** Root-caused the long-standing stim-pixel x/y flip in the contra→ipsi kernel figures. `loadUVt` returns `U_cp`/`mimg_cp` in the SAME native orientation as the trusted `pixelTuningCurveViewerSVD` (both `readNPY` the raw spatial components, no transpose). `params.pixel` is stored `[x=col, y=row]`: `py_prim=pixel(1)=col`, `px_prim=pixel(2)=row`, so getpixel_dFoF reads `mimg(px_prim±k rows, py_prim±k cols)` → recording site at array `(row=px_prim, col=py_prim)` (validated: predicting this footprint hits R²≈0.9 vs the real recorded `y_full`). BUT `contra_prediction`/`cp_kernel_explorer` display TRANSPOSED (`imagesc(mimg')`; in that frame screen-x=row, screen-y=col). Correct marker on a transposed display = `plot(px_prim,py_prim)` — which the explorer does (✓) but the static `[CP-KERNEL]` (lines 518,537) and `[CP-BLEED]` (671, dump 711–714) markers do `plot(py_prim,px_prim)` (native-frame code) → they land on the TRANSPOSE of the pixel the weight map actually predicts. Net: weight-map target is anatomically correct; the static/bleed MARKERS are flipped. No code changed yet (diagnosis only).
**Why:** User flagged "the ipsi kernel pointed-to is correct but the kernels shown are for x/y-flipped ipsi"; needed to know whether target or marker was wrong before editing scientific figures. Also: `params.pixel` itself can be flipped by load/save schemes, so the orientation must be re-derived from the DATA, not trusted from params.
**Next:** Build `cp_find_stim_site` — data-derived stim localization (trial-averaged peri-stim inhibition map → most-inhibited pixel, mirroring `pixelTuningCurveViewerSVD`), report in native `[row,col]`, compare vs `(px_prim,py_prim)` and its transpose to settle the flip; verify in `cp_kernel_explorer` (focal homotopic hot-spot = true site, diffuse = transpose); then centralize one `stim_pix` + display convention and fix the markers consistently.

### 2026-06-29 — CP-HEMI best/worst figs: overlay original (un-centred) prediction to expose negative raw R²
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/contra_prediction.m` `[CP-HEMI]` — both example (`h_fig3`) and best/worst (`h_fig4`) per-segment figures previously plotted ONLY each trace centred on its own mean (`ya-mean(ya)`, `yh-mean(yh)`), i.e. a pure SHAPE comparison, while the panel titles reported the RAW (offset-penalised) `h_segR2`. That mismatch hid why raw R² can go negative. Now each axis plots the **original un-centred** actual (black solid) + prediction (red solid) AND the centred shape traces (dashed grey/orange); added a legend to fig4. check_matlab_code clean (only the pre-existing line-248 try/catch alignment warning).
**Why:** User: the best/worst panels were "shifting and shape comparing" — wanted the original prediction shown too. Raw `h_segR2 = 1 − Σ(ya−yh)²/Σ(ya−mean ya)²` includes the absolute level offset between actual and prediction; the readout `h_aKern = scoresTr\sKernTr` has no intercept and regressors are z-scored over the whole test block, so per-3-s segment the predicted DC level is uncalibrated and drifts. When the level offset² exceeds the segment's own variance, raw R²<0 even with good shape — this is the documented raw-vs-baseline-removed gap (`h_kernPk` vs `h_poolB`). The new solid-vs-solid gap makes that offset visible per segment.
**Next:** none required; if the absolute level is judged scientifically irrelevant (it is — the residual pipeline removes a per-trial baseline), report the shape/baseline-removed R² as the headline and keep raw only as the conservative bound.

### 2026-06-29 — Autotune "wandering" root-caused: plot artifact + dead online cost (not a search bug)
**Changed/Found:** Investigated why autotune wandered despite an accept-if-lowered rule (source: `rainier/StLab_Rainier/Main_experiment.py:453–488`). Findings: (1) my earlier progression plot used `input_params` = every APPLIED probe (incl. 5-rep evals of REJECTED candidates) — not the accepted base; the accepted gains live in per-session `Kdata.npy`/`Kval.npy`. (2) The accept/reject logic is CORRECT and works where the cost was live: **10-25 e1 Kval 11.9→4.6 monotone; 03-17 Kval 16.6→15.1→12.3 then held → converged (0.068,0.064)**. (3) Where it misbehaved it's the COST SIGNAL: **12-19 Kval≡0** (`self.er` never populated → 0>0 false → every step accepted → random walk; = AUDIT.md B2); **10-25 e2 Kval stuck 5.01 at (0,0)** (nothing beat no-control → never moved). (4) Code regressions: cost downgraded from full-error-trace norm (RMS, backup/old `tune_data[ac,:]=er[...]`) to scalar `mean(er)` (offset-only, `Main_experiment.py:455`); MP refactor leaves `self.er≡0` (autotune dead now); fixed step, no annealing. New fig `controller-tuning/paper/images/tuning/autotune_accepted_trajectory.png` (Kdata/Kval).
**Why:** User: algorithm shouldn't wander (accept only if cost lowered) — asked to find why. Root cause is the online error feeding the cost, plus an analysis-plot artifact; not the search rule.

**Changed/Found:** Clarified (user question) that the loader's `y` for ALL sessions = the saved `states.csv` cache = the controller's ONLINE real-time kernel-mean dF/F, NOT recomputed from raw frames via `getdfof`/`getpixel_dFoF`. So 10-18's "no response" means the controller's *logged/controlled* signal had no dip and no dose-response — which could be (a) genuine biological non-response, OR (b) the online dF/F at `pixel[390,390]` not reading the responsive region that day. Distinguishing requires a getdfof recompute from raw, which is NOT available (no widefield.tar / blue SVD / frameTimes / pixel cache extracted; raw is in a 165 GB .rar). New evidence: 10-17 and 10-18 use the **identical** params (pixel=[390 390], laser=[360 410], kernel=10), and the 10-18 lightCommand is **as strong as 10-17** (max 4.79 V, median ~1.0 when on, ~17 % on-time) — so the controller tried hard but the controlled signal stayed flat. Reframed the `load_grid.m` hold note and softened the earlier "photoinhibition did not work (prep/site/power)" overclaim. For the cost-surface PURPOSE states.csv is the correct variable, so 10-18's exclusion stands regardless of cause.
**Why:** User correctly challenged the "dead response" wording and asked whether the signal came from getdfof or the saved cache.
**Next:** To settle biology-vs-pipeline for 10-18, extract the raw widefield from the .rar and recompute kernel-mean dF/F at pixel[390,390] via getdfof, then re-test for a stim-locked dip / dose-response. Only worth it if 10-18 is specifically needed.

### 2026-06-28 — Debugged AL_0034 10-18: no stim-locked inhibition (dead session); robustified blue-frame detection
**Changed/Found:** `controller-tuning/ct_process_set.m` — debugging 10-18 surfaced two things. (1) **Blue-frame detection bug:** per-frame `blueLEDmonitor` thresholding dropped ~406 ambiguous frames (detected 243247 vs states active 243653), causing cumulative onset drift → xcorr r≈0. Exposures strictly alternate B/V (99.8 %, max run=1), so switched `ct_timeline_onsets` to **parity-based** blue frames (`expRise(st:2:end)`, st seeded by which LED is on at exposure 1) — now matches states.csv length EXACTLY for both 10-17 (140975) and 10-18 (243653); 10-17 result unchanged (lag 72, r 0.24). (2) **10-18 has no usable inhibition signal even after the fix:** trial-avg window mean is **+0.66 at the best of all lags 0–140** (no dip anywhere), states pctile median 0 / IQR [−1.5,+1.7], and **no dose-response** — min-dF/F-in-window is ~−3 for every Kp level *including Kp=0* (Kp=0: −2.91 vs Kp=0.3: −2.90), i.e. pure noise floor, not stim. `Kr.npy` is 2×520 vs 460 IP rows vs 456 onsets (counts disagree too). Conclusion: photoinhibition did not work this session (prep/site/power) — NOT an alignment/code issue. **10-18 stays hold** with an evidence-based note.
**Why:** User asked to debug 10-18. Root-caused it past the earlier "barely regulates" guess to a genuinely non-responsive session; also fixed a real frame-detection bug that benefits all 7-col sessions.
**Next:** none for 10-18 (unusable). Grid set stays at 5 (4×AL_0033 + AL_0034 10-17). If another AL_0034 grid day with working opsin exists, it would slot in via the same adapter.

### 2026-06-28 — Resolved AL_0034 10-17 alignment: states.csv has a fixed ~2 s logging delay; auto-calibrated
**Changed/Found:** `controller-tuning/ct_process_set.m` — the "2 s early" stim onsets were NOT a detection error: trial-averaging the per-frame laser envelope vs dF/F (72 strong-gain trials) showed the **laser is on t=0–4 s but the states.csv dF/F dip only begins at t≈2.06 s** — non-physiological, so **states.csv is logged with a fixed ~71-frame (2.0 s) processing delay** relative to the laser/imaging clock. Confirmed by xcorr(laser-on envelope, −dF/F): clean single positive-lag peak at **71–72 frames**. Fix: `ct_timeline_onsets` now **auto-calibrates** this lag per session (xcorr, best positive lag) and adds it to the laser-derived onset; `ct_load_session` loads `y` before onsets so the adapter can use it; added `cfg.LAG_MAX=140`. Also `params.dur=4` for this session (laser 4 s, not 3). Result: 10-17 node traces now dip at t=0 and regulate ~−5; **Jmin 44 → 24** (comparable to AL_0033). **AL_0034 2024-10-17 e30 flipped to `ready`** — grid set is now 5 (4×AL_0033 + 1×AL_0034, cross-mouse). 10-18 stays hold (weak regulation, count mismatch).
**Why:** User confirmed from plots the stim was detected 2 s early; root-caused it to the states.csv logging delay rather than the onset detection.
**Next:** Decide whether to keep cost window [0 3] s for 10-17 (dur=4 there) or extend to [0 4]; sanity-check the auto-lag's modest xcorr r (0.24 full-session) doesn't misfire on future 7-col sessions (10-18 still excluded on other grounds).

### 2026-06-28 — 7-col AL_0034 Timeline onset adapter built; both sessions remain hold (alignment untrustworthy)
**Changed/Found:** Added `ct_timeline_onsets` to `ct_process_set.m` (7-col branch) + cfg knobs (`FS_TL=2000`, `LASER_THR=0.1`, `TRIAL_GAP_S=5`). Recovers trial onsets from the Timeline `lightCommand` laser channel (low abs threshold — 0.5×max misses small regulated pulses — merged with a >5 s ITI gap) and maps to the states.csv 35 Hz axis via blue-frame times (`widefieldExposure` rising edges gated by `blueLEDmonitor`). **Onset DETECTION is clean for 10-17 e30 (205 onsets = 205 IP rows, 666-frame/19 s period).** BUT alignment to states.csv fails: the states dip **lags the detected laser onset by ~2 s (75 frames)** with trial-to-trial jitter (first-y<−2 IQR [−12,69] frames); a constant +75-frame shift makes the dip immediate (−4.1 @0–0.5 s) but then it **rebounds to −3.1 mid-stim** — so no single offset gives clean regulation across the [0,3 s] window. Earlier "settled −4.4 @t=2–3 s" was a false positive (it caught the *late-arriving* dip, not a settled level). **10-18 e1** separately fails: controller barely regulates (settled 0.74, only 8 % of trials reach <−3), 456/460 onsets, blueFrames 243247 ≠ states 243653. **Decision: both 7-col sessions stay `status='hold'`;** adapter code retained (onset detection is correct; the states↔Timeline *anchor* is the unresolved piece). Grid analysis remains the 4 AL_0033 sessions.
**Why:** User asked to wire 10-17 and debug 10-18. Did both; the data does not support trustworthy alignment for either.
**Next:** ASK USER — is there a ~2 s pre-stim/baseline phase (or delayed laser ramp-to-effective-power) in the AL_0034 Oct-2024 protocol that would explain the states-dip lag? If the protocol defines the true stim onset relative to the lightCommand start, bake that lead into `ct_timeline_onsets`. Otherwise these two sessions are unusable for the cost surface.

### 2026-06-26 — State-dependence collapses under faithful prediction (delta effect was a prototype artifact)
**Changed/Found:** Regenerated `impulse-analysis/data/cp_statedep_explorer_AL_0033_0129_e1.mat` using `yhat_zw` (Zhiwen CP-HEMI K=200 rank98) instead of the OLS-K=50 prototype, so the interactive `cp_statedep_explorer` now reflects the faithful model. The |peak-deviation| state-dependence correlations (n=748) CHANGED: MOTION r=−0.002→+0.047 (p=0.20); VARIANCE r=+0.068→−0.027 (p=0.47); **DELTA r=+0.093 p=0.011 → r=−0.014 p=0.69**. The previously "significant" delta effect VANISHED. Interpretation: delta = slow-wave shared brain state; the weak OLS-K=50 model left delta-correlated prediction error in the residual, which masqueraded as state-dependence. The rank-98 field-RRR absorbs that shared variance → spurious correlation gone. **Under the proper pipeline, no significant |peak-dev| state-dependence on motion/variance/delta for this metric.**
**Why:** User asked whether the interactive plotter was using the faithful prediction (it wasn't — it read the pre-swap dump). Regenerating exposed that the state-dependence conclusion was prediction-quality-dependent.
**Next:** Relaunch explorer (`clear; close; cp_statedep_explorer(mat)`) to view faithful version. Re-test state-dependence with the SIGNED impulse-strength metric (not just |dev|) under yhat_zw before concluding no state-dependence. Re-run over-subtraction check under rank-98. Crystallize into a script; commit.

### 2026-06-25 — Clean-mode contra predictor: mode selection can't de-bias the impulse; over-subtraction is n.s.
**Changed/Found:** Prototyped in the base workspace (AL_0033 0129, K=50 contra modes; not yet a script). Goal: select stim-clean contra SVD modes to predict the primary pixel without over-subtracting the impulse. Findings: (1) full-mode contra→primary OLS readout test R²=0.885 (≈ CP-HEMI 0.898), so a direct mode→y readout is a faithful stand-in. (2) Per-mode stim-effect (max-amp [0,200]ms dev vs catch, perm z): max |z|=2.92, only 4/50 modes |z|>2, none >3 — the localized contra bleed dilutes across global modes, and the most stim-affected modes are the WEAKEST predictors. (3) Trial-avg over-subtraction (max-amp n=84): actual impulse −1.75 dF, full-model yhat leak −0.37 raw / **−0.32 catch-corrected (18.5%)**, but **z=−0.87, p=0.39, bootstrap 95% CI [−0.86,+0.21] straddles 0 — NOT significant.** (4) Mode selection cannot remove it: drop |z|>2 → R²=0.884, leak −0.28 (≈unchanged); drop the dominant global mode 1 → R² collapses 0.885→0.247 but leak stays −0.32; drop top-contrib modes → R²→0.13, leak WORSE (−0.45). OLS reconstructs the stim-correlated component from the remaining correlated modes.
**Why:** User asked to build a mechanism that picks the most-informative, least-stim-affected contra kernels to avoid over-subtraction. The prototype shows the premise doesn't hold: the contra prediction is not significantly biased w.r.t. the impulse, and mode selection structurally can't de-bias it (the leak is low-dim, correlated with the predictive structure, and reconstructable from any rich mode subset; removing it costs all the R²).
**Next:** Do NOT build the "mode cleanliness knob" explorer — it would imply a fixable bias that is neither significant nor selection-removable. Optional: package the diagnostic as a one-off robustness figure/function (over-subtraction estimate + CI + R²-vs-drop tradeoff) for the paper's methods/robustness. Revisit only if pooling sessions/all-amps raises power enough to make the 18% bias significant.
**Changed/Found:** `bilateral-analysis/cl_ol_single_session.m` — added `USE_LAST_N = 100` knob; per-side trial masks now AND in a `useMask` keeping only the last USE_LAST_N trials (`firstUse = nTrials-USE_LAST_N+1`), dropping session-start warm-up, and print the used range. On AL_0048 06-24 e5: drops trials 1–4 (all CL) → OL stays 51, CL 53→49; OL/CL MSE ratio 0.91 (all) → **1.01** (last 100), CL mean MSE 40.27→36.31. check_matlab_code clean.
**Why:** User: "only last 100 trials are actually supposed to be used for OL vs CL analysis" — the opening CL block (trials 1–7) is warm-up, including the onset-contaminated early trials.
**Next:** Last-100 keeps trial 5 (the −1.0 s early CL trial, now the first included CL trial); set `USE_LAST_N=99` if it should also be dropped. Confirm the "last 100" protocol holds for other AL_0048 sessions before reusing this value.

### 2026-06-25 — cl_ol_single_session: derive stim onsets from input_params(:,2) frame index − 2.918 s lead (fix 37 s misalignment)
**Changed/Found:** `bilateral-analysis/cl_ol_single_session.m` — replaced the rising-edge re-detection (`detectStimsFromInput`/`correctOnsets`/`min_idx`, all removed) with `d.stimStarts = d.timeBlue(round(input_params(:,ONSET_FRAME_COL))) - ONSET_LEAD_S` (`ONSET_FRAME_COL = 2`, `ONSET_LEAD_S = 2.918`). On AL_0048 2026-06-24 exp 5 the old path left onsets a uniform **−36.98 s** early: `correctOnsets`' "median nearest-neighbour residual" offset estimator **aliases** when the true offset (~37 s) exceeds half the ~11.4 s trial spacing (collapsed to +3.0 s instead of +37 s), and the input is continuously active for CL trials so rising-edge detection is meaningless there. col 2 is an imaging-frame index logged at TRIAL END, not onset: measured against the 51 OL input-pulse rising edges (the true laser-on for discrete OL pulses), col2 sits **2.918 s after** onset, std 0.009 s / IQR [2.917, 2.918] — extremely tight (≈ dur=3). OL/CL MSE ratio 0.72 (misaligned) → 0.91. check_matlab_code clean; re-ran end-to-end. User chose the measured 2.918 s over a flat 2 s.

### 2026-06-25 — Bleed explorer: max-amp-deviation mode; per-pixel FDR found structurally under-powered
**Changed/Found:** `utils/cp_bleed_explorer.m` — added a metric popup: 'dose-response slope' (existing) + 'max-amp deviation' Δ = mean(top-amp [post−baseline]) − mean(amp-0 catch [post−baseline]) (catch = per-pixel "no input" reference). Added per-window trial selection and extended the baseline slider to 2000 ms (default 1000). Max-amp Δ is much cleaner: short window [0,500] ms → BILATERAL inhibition (ipsi stim-site blob + a contralateral homotopic blob = the bleed); long window [0,~1200] ms → positive rebound. **BUT Significance = 0/164818 px, and that is a statistical artifact:** with nPerm=1000 the smallest achievable p is 1/1001≈1e-3, while BH-FDR across ~165k pixels needs the top pixels at p≲3e-7 — so per-pixel permutation+FDR cannot reject here regardless of effect size (same wall hit by the earlier [CP-BLEED] 0/80987). The maps are clearly real (coherent blobs, sensible physiology). check_matlab_code clean.
**Why:** User proposed max-amp-vs-catch deviation as a more sensitive/intuitive bleed measure; building + testing it exposed the per-pixel-FDR limitation.
**Next:** Replace per-pixel FDR with CLUSTER-BASED permutation (cluster mass vs a max-cluster null — few comparisons, 1000 perms suffice; standard imaging fix) and/or a permutation-calibrated parametric z. Pending user's call.

### 2026-06-24 — Repointed cp_residual_core "Global" to the [CP-HEMI] field→kernel predictor
**Changed/Found:** New `utils/cp_hemi_predictor.m` — factors the [CP-HEMI] whole-hemi RRR + field→kernel readout into a reusable AFFINE map on the contra modes: `yhat_dF(t) = V_c_full(1:K,t)'·bk + b0` (%dF/F, y_full units). Builds the ipsi SVD (cached), CanonCor2 on inter-stim spontaneous windows, kernel-mean readout `aKern = scores\kernel-mean`, picks the RRR rank by held-out primary-pixel R², and bakes TRAIN-stats z-scoring into (bk,b0) so it deploys pointwise to any frames. `utils/cp_residual_core.m`: added `opts.predictor` ('hemi' default | 'ols' legacy); when 'hemi' it builds the map and routes ALL 5 prediction sites through one `predFun(g)`, forces decontam OFF, sets `cv_mean = Hp.cv`. The OLS `beta_cp` path is fully preserved under 'ols'. `contra_residual.m` [CP-SETUP]: surfaced a `predictor` knob (default 'hemi'), passed via opts. check_matlab_code: cp_hemi_predictor + contra_residual CLEAN; cp_residual_core shows only pre-existing setup-var warnings (PS/mlag/t_trial/w_r/idx_r_cp/U_svd_cp), none from these edits.
**Why:** [CP-HEMI] is the single canonical contra predictor, so the residual's "Global" component must come from it, not the 200-mode OLS. KEY correctness points: residual = A − Pr with BOTH baseline-subtracted per trial → b0 cancels and the `/mI_kern·100` scaling puts Pr in absolute %dF/F = y_full units, so A−Pr is a genuine local residual; the shared 50-mode latents structurally can't absorb onset stim-bleed, so decontam is off (bleed rebuilt later). HEMI vs OLS single-pixel R² ~0.85 vs 0.898, but the shared-latent Global is the cleaner subtractor (less local leakage) — the scientific reason HEMI was chosen.
**Next:** RIG RUN to verify (could NOT execute — empty MATLAB workspace): run load_experiments → contra_residual [CP-SETUP] on AL_0033; confirm the printed HEMI held-out R² (~0.85) and re-read the state partials (Motion/PreVar/PreDelta) — the locked OLS result was PreVar partial +0.085 (p=0.02), Motion null. Then A/B vs `predictor='ols'` to quantify how the cleaner Global shifts the Local state-dependence. If numbers move materially, update FINDINGS.

AL_0034 = 7 cols: gains in `col2/col3`, `col4`=|ref|(=5), `col6`=0-based trial counter, **no onset-frame column** (my loader read col2 as onset → 0 → every trial skipped, Jmin=NaN; and read col5/col6 → got const 4 and the 0→204 trial counter = the "Ki 0–204" bug).

(3) Crash: empty `y` from mean_states (single-row, wrong column pick) hit `y(NaN)` in the align-check.
**Why:** The cache-first + fixed-column assumptions don't survive the heterogeneity. states.csv is logged by the controller for every session and avoids SVD/video entirely.

### 2026-06-24 — DECISION: [CP-HEMI] is the canonical contra→ipsi predictor; consolidation plan
**Changed/Found:** No code yet. Inventoried every contra-prediction pipeline and chose ONE canonical predictor: **[CP-HEMI]** (whole-hemi RRR, 50 shared modes, field→kernel primary-pixel readout). Full list — predictors: [CP-FIT] instantaneous OLS single-pixel (`beta_cp`, ~200 modes, CV R²≈0.898; the residual engine is the same model via `utils/cp_residual_core.m`), [CP-HEMI] (pooled 0.925), [CP-RRR] single-pixel rank sweep, [CP-4] contra+TF composite, `contra_lds.m` dynamic LDS/ERA; diagnostics/processing: [CP-2] kernel viz, [CP-BLEED]/[CP-BLEED-SD] bleed map, [CP-IMP] bleed-negation residual; consumer: `contra_residual.m` (state-dependence) via `cp_residual_core`.
**Why:** User wants a single main predictor. Chose HEMI for Zhiwen-fidelity and because its 50 SHARED latents are the principled "global" subtractor — a 200-mode single-pixel OLS can absorb stim-bleed/local structure and deflate the local residual. Accepts single-pixel R²~0.85 vs 0.898.
**Next (consolidation, pending go-ahead — deletes are irreversible):** (1) chuck redundant predictors — remove [CP-RRR] + [CP-4] sections, delete `contra_lds.m`; (2) decide fate of bleed/decontam diagnostics ([CP-2]/[CP-BLEED]/[CP-BLEED-SD]/[CP-IMP]) — HEMI's shared map sidesteps bleed-absorption, so the decontam may be unneeded; (3) preserve `all_starts_cp`/`pre_frames` (HEMI needs them) if [CP-FIT] is trimmed; (4) REPOINT `cp_residual_core`'s Global component to the HEMI field→kernel readout → re-derives all state-dependence numbers. Commit current HEMI state first.

### 2026-06-23 — [CP-HEMI] lever-1: spontaneous windows = post-settle inter-stim gaps
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/contra_prediction.m` [CP-HEMI] — replaced the short pre-stim spontaneous windows with the FULL gap between consecutive stim starts: drop `h_settle_s=1.0 s` after each stim for settling, use the rest (`[onset_j + 1s , onset_{j+1})`; last stim → tail to `nF_m`). New knobs `h_win_mode` ('interstim' default | 'prestim' legacy) + `h_settle_s`. Train/test still contiguous early/late on the time-sorted frame set; `h_maxFrm` cap unchanged. Diagnostic now prints gap count, total spont frames + seconds, train/test counts. check_matlab_code: 0 new issues.
**Why:** Lever-1 toward Zhiwen's 0.99 — he regresses one long contiguous spontaneous recording; we had only sub-second pre-stim snippets, which chop the slow global fluctuations that carry bilateral coherence. Inter-stim gaps give far longer windows while still excluding each stim's response. Per user instruction ("increase trial length, take time between stim starts, remove 1 s for settling, use the rest"). Amp-0 gap-fill events inside a gap are no-laser → kept as spontaneous.
**Next:** Run [CP-HEMI] on AL_0033 (selExp=3) with `h_win_mode='interstim'` vs `'prestim'` and compare pooled R²@rank10 — longer window lifts toward 0.99 ⇒ gap was timebase fragmentation; stays ~0.925 ⇒ intrinsic prep coherence ceiling (consistent with the uniform per-pixel R²). Raise `h_settle_s` if 1 s is too short for the response.

### 2026-06-23 — Decided AGAINST region restriction as the 0.925→0.99 lever (variance-weighted R² reasoning)
**Changed/Found:** No code change. Reconsidered (user push-back) whether restricting the ipsi target to sensory cortex (Zhiwen's `select_area`) would close pooled 0.925→0.99. Conclusion: NO — region restriction is a non-lever for THIS data, and recommending it contradicted our own 2026-06-22 result. Reasoning: pooled `sseExplainedCal` = variance-weighted mean of per-pixel R² (`R²_pool = Σ_p var(y_p)·R²_p / Σ_p var(y_p)`). RRR gives unpredictable pixels ~0 contra-covariance → they get ŷ≈0 and do NOT distort the canonical latents or good-pixel predictions (user's point, correct). A meaningless-weight pixel dilutes pooled R² ONLY in proportion to the variance it carries (its residual = its full variance), so only high-variance-AND-unpredictable pixels hurt. Our 06-22 test shows those are absent: dark/vessel exclusion barely moved pooled (0.925→0.927) and per-pixel R² is spatially UNIFORM (median 0.924, p10 0.852, p90 0.952). So 0.925 is a genuine uniform per-pixel predictability ceiling = intrinsic bilateral coherence/SNR of this prep, not a region-inclusion artifact.
**Why:** User: bad-region weights are forced meaningless by the regression, so region inclusion shouldn't hurt R². Correct for the FIT; the only exception (high-variance unpredictable pixels) is empirically absent here. My earlier "sensory restriction is the main 0.925→0.99 lever" (entry below) was wrong and self-contradictory with the 06-22 vessel-exclusion null — recorded as a corrected dead-end.
**Next:** Real levers to approach 0.99, re-ranked: (1) FRAME SET — Zhiwen regresses one contiguous spontaneous recording; we concatenate short pre-stim windows, which can discard the slow global fluctuations carrying bilateral coherence → test pooled on a contiguous spontaneous block if the session has one. (2) Hemodynamic-correction parity. (3) redoSVD SNR/frame-count. (4) DEFINITIVE: run our CanonCor2/sseExplainedCal port on Zhiwen's OWN figshare session (positive control) — reproduces ~0.99 ⇒ gap is prep, not code. Region restriction dropped.

### 2026-06-23 — [CP-HEMI] primary-pixel = field→kernel-average; audited vs Zhiwen source
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/contra_prediction.m` [CP-HEMI] — replaced report number (3) (the old RAW primary-pixel R^2: a direct latent→y_full OLS with fitted intercept) with a **field-then-kernel-average** readout, and verified the rest of CP-HEMI against Zhiwen's actual source `YE-et-al-2023-spirals/spirals_mirror/preprocessing/getReducedRankRegressionHEMI.m`. Final (Zhiwen-faithful) implementation: reconstruct the primary pixel's full-resolution recording-kernel mean straight from the ipsi SVD (`h_meanU = mean(U_ipsi_raw(kernel rows))`, `h_meanU*V_ipsi`), then read it out through the SAME canonical latents (`a_kern = scores\kernel-mean`; CanonCor2 scores are orthonormal on train so `a_kern(1:n)` == the field's `a(:,1:n)` truncation → this is genuinely "predict field, average kernel pixels", not a free OLS). Crucially this does NOT enter the RRR fit, so pooled (1) + per-pixel map (2) are byte-identical to before (≈0.925). Kernel footprint taken from `getpixel_dFoF`/`load_experiments` (square `rows=px_prim±k, cols=py_prim±k`, `k=d.params.kernel`); the `[CP-BLEED]` line-~783 iy/ix comment has that convention flipped — followed the getpixel ground truth. `/mean(mimg_kernel)*100` kept for interpretable %dF/F but it cancels in R^2. (Superseded an intermediate union/force-include design from earlier this turn — never run.) Added `k_prim` capture in setup + standalone `loadData` fallback. check_matlab_code: 0 new issues (17 flags all pre-existing).
**Why:** User: "be as close to Zhiwen's implementation as possible." AUDIT RESULT — our core already matches his: predictor = `zscore` of the **re-SVD'd contra-hemisphere** modes (our `V_c_full` = `redoSVD(U_contra,V_cp)` == his `zscore(Vleft)` from `redoSVD(UselectedLeft,V)`); target = re-SVD'd ipsi pixel field (== his `Uright*Vright`); `CanonCor2(signal',regressor')`; contiguous early-train/late-test; pooled `sseExplainedCal`; train/test z-scored independently. Zhiwen computes ONLY the pooled area number — the single primary pixel is OUR addition, so the most faithful thing is to extract it from his exact field, which is what (3) now does. MATH NOTE (in code): readout+kernel-avg both linear ⇒ predict-field-then-average == predict-the-kernel-mean ⇒ lands near the single-pixel ceiling (~0.85), NOT pooled 0.925; the 0.925 is a variance-weighted-pooling effect.
**Next:** Two real divergences from Zhiwen remain, both judgment calls for the user: (a) **sensory-area Allen restriction** — he `select_area(sensoryArea)` after atlas `imwarp`; we use the whole hand-drawn hemisphere + complement (no tform for AL_0033). This is the likely main driver of 0.925<0.99 (we pool over non-sensory/edge/vascular pixels he excludes); approximate with a tighter hand-drawn cortical-core ROI if pursued. (b) **frame set** — he uses a contiguous spontaneous recording (1:40000/40001:60000); we concatenate pre-stim windows (stim paradigm). Then run on AL_0033 (selExp=3; ipsi cache `impulse-analysis/data/AL_0033cp01291_svdraw_ipsi.mat` present) and report new (3) R^2@rank10.

### 2026-06-23 — cl_ol_single_session.m: robust per-trial onset correction (snap-or-fallback)
**Changed/Found:** `bilateral-analysis/cl_ol_single_session.m` — replaced the re-detection block's two branches (blind `numel(det)==nTr` 1:1 chronological assignment, and `det(1)-stimStarts(1)` constant-shift fallback) with a new `correctOnsets(ss0, det, SNAP_TOL)` helper. It estimates the findStims global offset robustly (median of each detected edge's nearest-neighbour residual to findStims onsets), shifts findStims by it, then snaps each trial to its nearest detected edge within `SNAP_TOL=0.5 s`, else keeps the offset-corrected findStims onset. Always returns one onset per `input_params` trial; prints snapped-vs-fallback counts.
**Why:** The old `numel(det)==nTr` path mis-assigns trials *silently* if detection misses one real edge and adds one spurious edge (count still matches but all later trials shift onto the wrong onset) — a real risk with oscillating CL controller output crossing `IN_THRESH` past the 2 s refractory. The `det(1)`-based offset was also poisoned by a single spurious first edge. The new method is robust to a minority of bad edges and degrades gracefully per trial. Verified faithful to canonical windowing/MSE (`load_sessions.m`: n_pre=105, n_win=210, n_err=105, `norm(seg-ref)`).
**Next:** Re-run on AL_0048 2026-06-20 exp 2; check the console "%d snapped / %d kept fallback" line — if many fall back, detection threshold/refractory or single-channel selection needs revisiting.

### 2026-06-23 — FOUND: stimStarts offset ~37 s — findStims mode-1 horizon subtraction (stale zero-buffer)
**Changed/Found:** AL_0048 2026-06-20 controller session: `d.stimStarts(1)=42.41 s` but the real first input (d.inpVals638/594 rising edge) is at 79.44 s. Root cause: `initialize_data` calls `findStims(d,1)`; mode 1 computes `t(input_params(:,2) - horizon)` with `horizon=40*35=1400` samples (~40 s), which assumes a leading zero-buffer the file no longer has → all onsets land ~37–40 s early. `utils/findStims.m` is do-not-modify. Added a re-detection overlay to `bilateral-analysis/debug_stimstarts.m` (knobs `SHOW_DETECTED`/`IN_THRESH`/`MIN_GAP`): builds a combined input from IN_FIELDS, takes rising edges > IN_THRESH with a MIN_GAP refractory, draws them magenta, and prints first-onset offset vs stimStarts(1).
**Why:** Confirm visually that input-derived onsets (mode-0 style) align with the command/dF while the mode-1 onsets are offset, before overriding stimStarts in the analysis.
**Next:** If the magenta onsets sit on the input edges, add the same rising-edge detection to `cl_ol_single_session.m` to override `d.stimStarts`/`d.stimEnds` after `initialize_data` (do not edit findStims). Then re-run CL/OL analysis.

### 2026-06-22 — [CP-HEMI] RESULT: whole-hemi prediction plateaus at 0.925 (vs Zhiwen 0.99)
**Changed/Found:** Ran [CP-HEMI] on AL_0033 (selExp=3) via MATLAB MCP. Built ipsi SVD (`AL_0033cp01291_svdraw_ipsi.mat`, ~7 min redoSVD). Results: POOLED ipsi-hemi R²=**0.925@rank10, 0.931 peak@rank50**; RAW primary-pixel R²=0.856@rank10/0.861 (vs single-pixel 200-mode OLS 0.899); per-pixel median 0.924 (p10 0.852, p90 0.952). Rank curve reproduces Zhiwen Fig-3C shape (plateau by rank~10). Exported `paper/cp_hemi_rank.png` + `cp_hemi_r2map.png`. Also fixed legend bug in source (`ItemTokenSize` must be set on the legend object, not passed as name-value). Confirmed data loads "corrected temporal components" (hemo-corrected → parity OK).
**Why:** Gate `2026-06-22.8` — is our contra→ipsi predictor as good as Zhiwen's ~0.99? Answer: METHOD faithfully replicated (CanonCor2 RRR, 50 modes, whole-hemi target, sseExplainedCal), but pooled R²=0.93, ~0.065 short of 0.99. Tested vessel exclusion (dark-pixel >p40/>p60 cut): pooled barely moves (0.925→0.927) and per-pixel R² is uniform → the gap is NOT a vasculature/edge artifact, it's intrinsic bilateral-coherence/SNR of this prep. Slightly below the gate's 0.95 floor.
**Next:** Definitive validation = run our CanonCor2/sseExplainedCal port on Zhiwen's OWN session as positive control (needs his figshare data) to prove gap is data-not-code. Optional: tighter cortical-core ROI (away from window edge/sinus). Decide with Nick whether 0.93 suffices to proceed to the residual/state-dependence analysis.

### 2026-06-22 — cl_ol_single_session.m: robust pixel resolution (fix out-of-frame index error)
**Changed/Found:** `bilateral-analysis/cl_ol_single_session.m` — `pixelDFoF` threw `Index in position 1 is invalid` (`U(y-k:y+k,...)` with `y-k<1`) because the plain `mode()` over a side's `input_params(:,15:16)` picked up placeholder rows (OL/non-targeted trials carry pixel `[0 0]`), giving `y≈0`. Added helper `resolvePixel` that drops non-positive/non-finite coord rows, takes the modal valid (x,y), validates it sits inside the SVD frame with a kernel margin, auto-swaps x↔y if only the transpose is in-frame, and errors with the side's unique pairs + frame size if neither fits. Frame size detected from `d.svd.mimg`/`U`; added a bounds guard inside `pixelDFoF`; richer per-side console diagnostic.
**Why:** First rig run failed on the controlled-pixel reconstruction; the mode was contaminated by zero-placeholder trials. Filtering to in-frame coords recovers the true per-side controlled pixel, and the clear error path distinguishes the "cols 15/16 are mm/downsampled, not pixels" case.
**Next:** Rerun on rig; if it still errors, read the printed unique (x,y) pairs + `SVD frame HxW` to confirm cols 15/16 are raw image pixels in [x=col, y=row] (vs mm/downsampled) and adjust `PIX_X_COL`/`PIX_Y_COL` or add a mm→pixel conversion.

### 2026-06-22 — Add [CP-HEMI] whole-hemisphere RRR; faithful Zhiwen/Ye replication
**Changed/Found:** New `%% [CP-HEMI]` section in `impulse-analysis/contra_prediction.m` (inserted before [CP-BLEED]) + vendored `utils/CanonCor2.m` (cell variant) and `utils/sseExplainedCal.m` from Ye et al. 2023 `spirals_mirror`. Faithful port of his `getReducedRankRegressionHEMI.m`: predict the WHOLE ipsi-hemisphere pixel field from h_K=50 z-scored contra SVD modes via reduced-rank regression (CanonCor2), instantaneous, contiguous early-train/late-test split, scored with sseExplainedCal. Outputs (1) pooled ipsi-hemi R² vs rank (Zhiwen-comparable, target ~0.99), (2) per-pixel R² map, (3) RAW primary-pixel R² vs rank through the same shared latents. Exports `paper/cp_hemi_rank.png` + `cp_hemi_r2map.png`. check_matlab_code clean on all three (no new warnings in contra_prediction.m).
**Why:** Meeting action `2026-06-22.8` — validate predictor reaches Zhiwen's ~0.95–0.99 at rank ~10 before extending residual/state-dependence. Read his source (repo one folder up): his 0.99 is POOLED variance-explained over the whole right hemisphere (all sensory pixels × time), NOT a single pixel. Our existing single-pixel CV R²≈0.90 (CP-FIT) is a different, harder quantity and structurally cannot reach 0.99 — confirmed by his code, not just suspected. CP-HEMI reproduces his target/metric so the comparison is honest; primary pixel falls out as one column.

### 2026-06-20 — Decided AGAINST widening inhibition-energy window to 0-300 ms (keep 0-200 ms)
**Changed/Found:** Tested 0-200 vs 0-300 ms dip window on AL_0033 residual stream (R in workspace). Dip TROUGH = 114 ms (actual AND residual) — fully inside 0-200 ms. The 200-300 ms window is NOT more inhibition: ACTUAL mean [0-200]=−1.280 vs [200-300]=**+0.349** (post-dip REBOUND, opposite sign); RESIDUAL [0-200]=−0.268 vs [200-300]=+0.014 (flat). So 0-300 ms would average the dip with the rebound → dilutes actual inhibition energy by ~27% and conflates inhibition with rebound. State partials robust to window (Var 0.085→0.103, Delta 0.073→0.076, Motion null both) — 0-300 only nudges Var significance because dev_stim is a variability metric gaining scatter from recovery samples, not more inhibition.
**Why:** User asked whether to switch inhibition energy to 0-0.3 s.
**Decision:** KEEP 0-200 ms (peak_mode=3, the locked project-wide inhibition-energy def used by dose_response/motion_analysis/paper). 0-300 ms mixes in the rebound and breaks cross-analysis consistency; dev_stim window is non-critical so no reason to change. NOTE: `neg_win=[0 0.30]` is the bleed-subtraction window, NOT the energy window — no conflict. If the rebound is of interest, give it its OWN metric (e.g. mean 0.2-0.5 s).
**Next:** none (window stays 0-200 ms).

### 2026-06-20 — New [CP-LOCAL] stream in contra_residual.m: fig-6 predictability is GLOBAL (contra), NOT local
**Changed/Found:** Added `%% [CP-LOCAL]` section to `impulse-analysis/contra_residual.m` + flipped its `use_motion` default true→false (motion must stay OUT of the contra map or it is partly regressed out of the local residual, biasing the motion state-test; variance/delta results identical either way, R²=0.899 vs 0.900). The section ports motion_analysis.m fig-6 metric (`|dip − amp-mean|`, the single-trial deviation from the trial-average = assumed low-dim LTI/SISO impulse response) and SPLITS it: dA=actual ipsi (=fig6, global+local mixed), dG=contra prediction (GLOBAL incoming network activity), dL=residual (LOCAL stim effect, contra removed). DV |dev| z-within-amp. RAN selExp=3 (AL_0033, n=748). RESULT — the predictability state-dependence is GLOBAL, not local:
  - **Motion**: Actual rho=−0.001 (p=0.98), Global −0.004 (p=0.91), Local +0.033 (p=0.37). fig-6 High-motion(z>0.5,n=58) vs rest median |dev|: Actual −0.435/−0.302 (p=0.07, the fig-6 effect, weak here), Global −0.286/−0.278 (p=0.15), **Local −0.212/−0.274 (p=0.83, sign REVERSED)**. → removing contra-predicted global activity ABOLISHES the motion-predictability effect. Fig 6 is a global/network-state effect, not a local stim-response property.
  - **Variance**: Actual +0.428, Global +0.440, **Local +0.143 (p=1.3e-4)**. Variance also mostly global, but the LOCAL effect keeps a small genuine variance dependence (matches CP-RES partial 0.085, p=0.02). This is the ONLY state-dependence the isolated local effect retains.
  Exported `paper/images/figure2/cp_local_state.png` (2 rows motion/var × 3 cols actual/global/local). check_matlab_code: 0 issues.
**Why:** User's framing — trial-average dynamics ≈ low-dim LTI SISO on ipsi; trial-level TF fit infeasible on the real nonlinear system; so use contra to predict GLOBAL activity incoming to the ipsi kernel, isolate the LOCAL stim effect, and test its state-dependence. Wanted the fig-6 "heavy motion → closer to average" analysis redone on the local-only (contra-residual) signal.
**Conclusion:** The fig-6 motion-predictability lives in the GLOBAL (contra-shared) component; the isolated LOCAL stim effect is NOT motion-state-dependent (and the high-motion sign even flips). The local effect's only retained state-dependence is a weak pre-stim-variance one (+0.14 raw, +0.085 partial). So "local stim effect is motion-steerable" is NOT supported; "local effect is weakly variance-dependent" is.
**Next:** Replicate [CP-LOCAL] on AL_0041 (e1/e2, needs ROI draw). Consider variance-quantile dose-response on the LOCAL deviation (dL) specifically; and a signed variance decomposition (var(dA)=var(dG)+var(dL)+2cov) to quantify the global/local split of total trial variability.

### 2026-06-20 — Steerability = a VARIANCE axis (RAW response); motion steerability is variance-mediated — bridges controller MSE
**Changed/Found:** Tested the controller's "motion trials → lower MSE → steerable" in the impulse study using the RAW actual stim response (NOT the contra residual — that regresses motion out + absorbs the state structure). Metric = per-trial deviation from amplitude-mean response (open-loop MSE analog), z-within-amp. AL_0033, n=748. **VARIANCE = the steerability axis:** dip-window (0-200 ms) MSE rho=+0.428, full-window (0-1 s) MSE rho=+0.682 (both p≈0); var|motion partial +0.444 (p=2e-37) — ~3× bigger than the contra-residual dev_stim (+0.145), which had been eating it. **MOTION steerable but variance-MEDIATED:** windowMSE rho=−0.082 (p=0.026, moving→lower MSE); dip MSE continuous null (+0.002) but THRESHOLD split high(z>1.5,n=31) vs low median devA −0.472 vs −0.384 (ranksum p=0.007) — matches locked motion-threshold finding + controller motThresh=1.5. At fixed variance motion adds nothing: partial(MSE, motion|var)=+0.099 (p=0.007, slightly WORSE on dip); indirect motion→var→MSE = −0.20×0.43 = −0.086 ≈ entire raw effect. dip-DEPTH (gain) vs motion −0.057 (ns).
**Why:** User clarified the real claim — controller shows motion→lower MSE→steerable; wants the impulse analog as state-dependence, with variance as a SEPARATE state expected to show higher prediction error. Required the correct substrate (raw response, not motion-regressed contra residual).
**Conclusion:** ONE arousal/desynchronization axis. Pre-stim variance is the strong NEURAL readout of steerability (high var → high open-loop response MSE → poor steerability); motion is a weak BEHAVIORAL proxy (steerable only because it indexes low variance; threshold effect). The impulse study supplies the MECHANISM for the controller's motion-MSE benefit (OL response reproducibility is variance-governed; motion marks the low-variance state). Use RAW-response deviation for the state/steerability question; reserve the contra residual for local-magnitude/dose-response.
**Next:** Replicate on AL_0041. Deepen variance axis: (1) variance-quantile MSE dose-response figure; (2) substitute delta/synchronization for raw variance; (3) separate gain (dip depth) vs reproducibility (noise) for variance; (4) within-amp shape vs noise; optionally TF-model residual as MSE once tf_fit run.

### 2026-06-20 — Mediation test: "motion→controllable via low variance" story REFUTED; carrier is neural variance
**Changed/Found:** Tested the proposed narrative (movement states are low-variance → stim response lands closer to expected → more "controllable") by calling `cp_residual_core(allExperiments,3,...)` directly and decomposing the paths on AL_0033 (n=748). Premise CONFIRMED: motion vs PreVar rho=−0.197 (p=6e-8), vs PreDelta −0.155 (p=2e-5) — moving = low-variance, desynchronized. Controllability concept CONFIRMED for variance: PreVar→dev_stim +0.145, and the stim-specific slice survives the dev_pre control (partial 0.087, p=0.02). BUT motion link REFUTED: motion→dev_stim direct +0.015 (p=0.68, null), motion|var partial +0.022 (p=0.56, null); var|motion partial +0.147 (≈unchanged from 0.145) → variance does NOT act through motion. Implied indirect motion→dev_stim via variance = −0.197×0.145 = −0.029 (too small to detect at n=748, and observed +0.015 is the wrong sign). Conclusion: controllability axis = intrinsic neural variance/synchronization state; motion is a lossy correlate (shares ~4% variance) and must NOT be the variable in the claim.
**Why:** User proposed writing the result as "motion is more amenable to stim prediction (low variance → close to prediction → controllable)." Needed to verify the mediation before adopting the framing.
**Next:** Adopt the controllability framing on pre-stim VARIANCE/delta, not motion. Replicate on AL_0041 before stating firmly; stim-specific partial is modest (0.087) so most of the raw var–dev_stim link is shared general prediction quality.

**Changed/Found:** Ran `cp_state_batch` (sessions=3, `decontam=false`, `use_motion=true`) on AL_0033 2025-01-29 via the MATLAB MCP — the "step back from stim-bleed negation, take leftover as ipsi-only local effect" reframe. Held-out contra→ipsi R²=0.900. Raw `β·contra` absorbs **79% of the dip** (per-amp 59–97%) so the no-negation residual recovers only **21%** of the dip magnitude (lower bound); amp-0 control clean (act +0.018 / pred +0.014). For both var/delta, dev_pre (no-stim control) is MORE state-dependent than dev_stim (0.251>0.145, 0.228>0.130) → most apparent state-dependence is contra-prediction QUALITY, not stim-response biology. res_E magnitude vs motion/var/delta = −0.008/+0.126/+0.161. Numbers BYTE-IDENTICAL to the decontam=ON run, confirming the bleed template cancels in within-amp deviations. Exported `paper/images/figure2/cp_state_batch.png`.

### 2026-06-20 — lds_ipsi.m rewritten: ERA (Ho-Kalman) realization, n4sid dropped entirely
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/lds_ipsi.m` now identifies the SISO laser→ipsi impulse response via ERA (empirical impulse response → Hankel SVD → A,B,C,D), with AR(p=10) least-squares for spontaneous timescales. NO System Identification Toolbox: n4sid's sssim MEX crashed a 4th time (identical Access violation in sssim→mkl dcopy) even with NaN guards. KEY EVIDENCE: reproduced the real-data scale on SYNTHETIC data via MCP (157,080 samples, 748 windows, horizon 20, autonomous) → ran CLEAN (|eig|max=0.96). So n4sid fails ONLY on real dF — a finite-but-degenerate (flat/gap-filled) window survives isfinite and makes n4sid's internal solve rank-deficient → sssim segfault. ERA uses only svd/eig/hankel/\ (stress-tested safe). Verified ERA core via MCP: recovers known eigenvalues exactly, IR reconstruction err 3e-15. check_matlab_code: 0 issues.
**Why:** Four crashes (hang→OOM→segfault→segfault) all traced to the n4sid MEX. ERA is robust to degenerate data (truncates small singular values), needs no iddata/sssim, and is better-motivated ("realize the impulse-response dynamics directly"). Drops flat windows (std<1e-9) defensively.
**Next:** Run `load_experiments; lds_ipsi` (selExp=3,n_states=5). Read held-out test dip R² + A_spont (AR) vs A_evoked (ERA) taus; check ERA singular-value spectrum implies ~n_states order. Then n_states sweep, compare OL response to tfest. For v2 (MIMO contra+motion), generalize ERA to MIMO (block-Hankel) or use the same svd-based realization — still avoid n4sid.

### 2026-06-20 — Ruled OUT MKL/CPU illegal-instruction bug as lds_ipsi crash cause (red herring)
**Changed/Found:** Checked the hypothesis that the n4sid crash was MathWorks issue 1043644 (AMD Jaguar "Illegal Instruction" in MKL linalg). Does NOT apply: machine is Intel Comet Lake (crash dump Family 6 Model 165 GenuineIntel), MKL 2024.1 AVX2 (CNR) path, and the original fault was an Access Violation (null-ptr dcopy), not an Illegal Instruction. Verified via MCP: deliberate MKL stress (gemm+svd+dcopy ×20 on 800²) ran clean in 1.5 s; synthetic n4sid (driven+autonomous) also clean. MKL is healthy.
**Why:** Crash reproduces only on real dF, never on clean synthetic data of the same shape/ops → data-triggered (non-finite dF), not a CPU/MKL dispatch bug. No code change this entry — hypothesis elimination only.
**Next:** Run hardened lds_ipsi after load_experiments. If it still crashes, distinguish by the dump header: Access violation = NaN guard missed a path (e.g. internally-generated NaN from degenerate-but-finite segment); Illegal Instruction = revisit MKL (try maxNumCompThreads(1)) — but unlikely given stress test passes.

### 2026-06-19 — Design: two observability versions of the LDS (SISO ipsi-only vs MIMO ipsi+contra+motion), distinct from existing fork-A build

V1 = SISO `u→y_ipsi` (control-faithful: PI controller fed back on ipsi; OL sim = tfest TF). V2 = MIMO output `[ipsi,contra,motion]` — motion as OUTPUT (readout of latent state) to raise observability. Each version identified with its OWN output set ⇒ correct `K` per version, no column-dropping.
**Why:** The scientific question is whether extra sensing (contra+motion) increases observability/estimation of the latent state beyond ipsi alone. Quantify via observability Gramian eigenspectrum (`gram(sys,'o')`), steady-state Kalman error cov `P∞` (`idare`) trace ratio + ipsi one-step prediction variance, and single-trial dip R² + CP-RES state partials on the innovation. Nick (MEETINGS.md:135) flagged SS≠TF for MIMO: holds — V1 SISO≡TF, V2 genuine MIMO.
**Next:** On user confirm, refactor `contra_lds.m` to an `obs_mode` switch ('siso'|'mimo', keep fork-A held-out as 3rd mode) sharing iddata/n4sid/KF; add `LDS-OBS` section (Gramian + P∞ comparison). Keep A_spont vs A_evoked timescale test (GCaMP-vs-neural control). Build only; user runs section-by-section. ### 2026-06-19 — Scaffold contra_lds.m: universal input-driven LDS + Kalman filter (LDS-SETUP through LDS-KF)
**Changed/Found:** `impulse-analysis/contra_lds.m` (new, ~260 lines) — 5 sections:
  - LDS-SETUP: load SVD/ROI cache (shared with cp_residual_core), build Z_obs [nF_m × nSV_lds], u_full (amplitude at stim onset frames, 0 elsewhere)
  - LDS-SPONT: n4sid on pre-trial 6 s windows (no input) → A_spont + spontaneous time constants
  - LDS-DRIVEN: n4sid on multi-trial windows [−1, +2.5] s with u = amplitude at onset → A_evoked, B, C, D, K; 80/20 train/test; open-loop dip R² on test set
  - LDS-OL: open-loop simulation (no Kalman) vs per-amplitude trial averages → exported lds_ol_impulse.png
  - LDS-KF: universal Kalman predictor over FULL session (not per-trial reset); contra-only update (K_c = K[:,1:nSV_lds]); ipsi held out; extracts per-trial baseline-corrected predictions; reports KF dip R²; saves cache `lds_{mn}_{MMDD}_e{en}_n{n}.mat`
**Why:** Static OLS map (R²≈0.90) hit ceiling; weak PreVar state effect (partial ρ≈0.085) may be residual prediction-quality bias. SS model trained on driven data (B captures impulse response, not inferable from spont). Universal KF: state at each trial onset reflects full session contra history — no artificial reset. Fork A: contra=observation, ipsi=held-out; K_c drops ipsi column so filter never observes the output being evaluated.
**Next:** Run LDS-SETUP → LDS-KF on selExp=3 (AL_0033); sweep n_states=[5 10 15 20]; check stability warnings. Then add LDS-STATE (partialcorr of KF residual vs motion/var/delta, same test as CP-RES) and LDS-EIG (complex z-plane eig(A_spont) vs eig(A_evoked)).

### 2026-06-19 — Strategy: INPUT-DRIVEN state-space model of the impulse response + Kalman in-trial disturbance rejection
**Changed/Found:** No code yet — design clarified after user correction. CORRECT design (supersedes my earlier disturbance-detection misread): fit an input-driven stochastic SS model `x⁺=Ax+Bu+w; z_c=C_c x+v; y=c_y'x+d·u+v` on the DRIVEN trials so the deterministic part IS the stim impulse response (= MIMO/stochastic generalization of the existing tfest model, and the generative unification of the contra-residual). Kalman filter's job is NOT to remove stim — it uses contra observations to reject per-trial disturbances (nonlinear/state-dependent deviations) from the nominal linear response; it's the optimal linear observer, rejecting only what's observable through contra. Validated user's key argument: spontaneous-only training has nothing in the MEAN to learn (E[x]→0, zero-mean stable) → must train on the response; refinement: spontaneous still constrains A (fluctuation–dissipation), only B/d need driven data.
**Why:** Static map near-ceiling on spontaneous (R²≈0.90), lags proven to hurt, current state effects weak (PreVar partial ρ≈0.087, PreDelta ρ≈0.077). Input-driven SS gives a controllable plant for the MPC motivation (FINDINGS), and a decisive test: compare eig-timescales of A_spont vs A_evoked vs A_per-state — equality ⇒ clean LTI/relaxation (also the GCaMP-vs-neural control: if A is just indicator decay they're trivially equal); inequality/state-variation ⇒ the state-dependent-dynamics finding.
**Next:** Resolve the in-trial observation fork with user (A: contra obs / ipsi held-out [recommended]; B: contra+early-ipsi online/MPC; C: contra as measured input ≈ tfest). Then scaffold `impulse-analysis/contra_lds.m` for fork A: n4sid/ssest with exogenous u=laser waveform (physical units, 'EnforceStability'), spontaneous pre-fit to init A, compare open-loop impulse response to tfest, report contra-Kalman single-trial pred error vs motion/var/delta. Replicate across 3 sessions. Refs: Semedo 2019 Neuron, Semedo 2022 Sci Adv, Linderman 2017 rSLDS.

### 2026-06-19 — Bleed comp is MATHEMATICALLY irrelevant to the residual STATE analysis (cancels in within-amp deviation)
**Changed/Found:** `contra_residual.m` — added `use_motion` + presettable `decontam` + `make_inspector` guards. Ran 3 conditions on selExp=3:
  - motion-free + comp (prior): PreVar partial ρ=0.085 (p=0.022), PreDelta 0.073 (p=0.051).
  - **motion + comp:** R²=0.900 (vs 0.899); PreVar partial 0.087 (p=0.020), PreDelta 0.077 (p=0.040).
  - **motion + NO comp:** PreVar partial 0.087 (p=0.0204), PreDelta 0.077 (p=0.0402), res_E vs var/delta +0.126/+0.161 — **BYTE-IDENTICAL to motion+comp**.
  PROOF: bleed comp subtracts a per-AMPLITUDE CONSTANT template (kernel_alpha·art_tap, same for every trial of an amp). dev_stim/dev_pre (deviation from amp-average) and z-within-amp res_E all subtract the amp-mean → the constant CANCELS exactly. So every within-amp state correlation is invariant to bleed comp. Comp changes ONLY absolute magnitude: dip recovery 97% (comp) vs 21% (no comp); no-comp residual traces SMALLER but SMOOTHER (boundary 0.055 vs 0.327, no decontam noise). Motion-in-baseline: negligible (R² +0.001, state corrs ~unchanged) — contra modes already carry motion.
**Why:** User overruled the earlier "motion stays out" diagnostic (controller + non-contra evidence) and asked whether bleed comp is needed at all since the real question is state-dependence of local effects.
**Decision:** STATE-dependence pipeline = `decontam=false`, `use_motion=true` (identical result, cleaner traces, no noisy decontam). Bleed comp (Stage-2 pooled) reserved ONLY for the absolute dose-response (amp vs res_E in physical units). Supersedes the prior "don't add motion" entry (motion now IN as a harmless nuisance regressor).
**Next:** Replicate across sessions (AL_0041). For dose-response only, implement Stage-2 pooled bleed. State result stands: var partial ~0.087 (p=0.02), delta ~0.077 (p=0.04) survive dev_pre control; motion null; bulk of raw effect = prediction quality.

### 2026-06-19 — Decided AGAINST re-adding motion to baseline: motion is not the prediction-error driver
**Changed/Found:** Diagnostic on contra_residual wide traces (selExp=3): residual energy in NO-bleed windows vs motion — pre-stim rho=−0.085 (p=0.02, weak NEGATIVE), **post-stim (0.4–1s) rho=−0.010 (p=0.78, NULL)**; high-motion trials (z>1.5, n=31, 4.1%) median post-stim resE=0.486 vs rest 0.484 (identical). So contra prediction is NOT degraded by motion, even post-stim (no bleed). The worst-dev inspector trial (#667) being high-motion was an outlier coincidence. Motion anti-correlates with arousal (vs var rho=−0.197, vs delta rho=−0.155) → movement=desynchronized/aroused=low delta. Earlier motion-in-baseline gave R²=0.898 vs 0.899 (no gain). REAL cause of poor stim-window prediction = the noisy per-amp decontam (Stage 1: sign-flips, −103% at A=0.5), injected only in 0–0.4s → fix is Stage 2 pooled bleed, NOT motion.
**Why:** User observed (via inspector) poor contra prediction in the stim window on a high-motion trial and proposed re-adding motion to the baseline predictors. Evidence contraindicates: no prediction benefit + reintroduces the motion-as-regressor-AND-test circularity.
**Next:** Implement Stage 2 (pooled amplitude-independent bleed) to clean the stim-window residual. Optionally test contra history (pX>0) to lower the R²=0.90 baseline floor. Motion stays OUT of the baseline.

### 2026-06-19 — CP-RES: stim-response "state-dependence" is mostly prediction-quality; weak variance effect survives
**Changed/Found:** `contra_residual.m` — added `%% [CP-RES]` section: per-trial residual DVs (res_E = 0-200ms residual energy; dev_stim = mean-sq deviation of residual from amp-avg over 0-200ms; **dev_pre** = same over 0.2s PRE-onset = contra-prediction-quality control) + motion/var/delta covariates; **partialcorr(dev_stim, state | dev_pre)** as the decisive test. DVs z-within-amp, covariates session-z, motexcl(z>1.5) for var/delta. RAN selExp=3 (AL_0033):
  - **Motion: NULL** — dev_stim rho=−0.004 (p=0.91), dev_pre ns, partial ns. Directly CONTRADICTS the "high motion → more predictable" hypothesis.
  - **PreVar:** dev_stim rho=+0.147 (p=8e-5); **dev_pre rho=+0.264 (p=9e-13) — LARGER**; PARTIAL(stim|pre) rho=+0.085 (**p=0.022, survives**). Small genuine stim-specific effect remains.
  - **PreDelta:** dev_stim rho=+0.130 (p=5e-4); dev_pre rho=+0.239 (p=1e-10); PARTIAL rho=+0.073 (**p=0.051, does NOT survive**). Effect explained by prediction quality.
  - res_E magnitude: motion −0.006, var +0.125, delta +0.158.
  - **HEADLINE:** dev_pre (no-stim prediction error) is MORE state-dependent than dev_stim for both var and delta → the apparent "stim response is state-dependent" is largely **contra-prediction QUALITY**, not stim-response biology. Only pre-stim variance keeps a small significant partial effect; delta and motion do not. The stim response itself is comparatively state-robust. Exported cp_res_state.png.
**Why:** Decisive test for the residual-reframing claim (JOURNAL 2026-06-17). The dev_pre control separates true stim-response state-dependence from baseline-prediction-quality state-dependence — without it the claim would be inflated (raw var rho=0.15, delta=0.13).
**Next:** Replicate across sessions (selExp 1,2 = AL_0041) — single-session so far. Decide if the weak variance partial (rho=0.085) is worth featuring. Stage-2 pooled bleed still needed for the amp-vs-res_E dose-response.

### 2026-06-18 — Collab README rewritten as standalone collaborator doc; fixed ΔF/F units error
**Changed/Found:** `collab_export/README.md` — rewrote as a self-contained collaborator-facing document (what the dataset is, file/units tables, trial table incl. catch trials, Python+MATLAB load recipes). **Caught a units bug** in the prior draft: it claimed the SVD was already in ΔF/F. Verified against the data that `U·V` is the mean-subtracted hemodynamic-corrected fluorescence **deviation (ΔF)**; **ΔF/F (%) = (U·V)/meanImage·100** (brain-pixel std ≈2%, p99 ≈8.6%). ### 2026-06-17 — Stage 1 bleed-spatial-decay GATE: separable+decays but NOT amp-scaled → use pooled bleed
**Changed/Found:** `contra_prediction.m` — added `%% [CP-BLEED-SD]` Stage-1 diagnostic (per-amplitude contra bleed maps → distance-from-stim, rank-1 separability, amplitude scaling). RAN on selExp=3 (AL_0033). Results:
  - **Separable: YES** — PC1 = 91.6% of the [pixel × amp] bleed (one spatial pattern scaled across amps).
  - **Decays from stim: YES (moderate)** — pooled |profile| vs distance-from-stim Spearman rho=−0.392 p≈0; beats distance-from-midline (rho=−0.204) → organized around the STIM site, not callosal/midline entry. λ≈464 px (gradual); per-amp curves PEAK at ~200–250 px (a localized contra locus), not monotonic from nearest pixel.
  - **Amplitude-scaled: NO** — blob bleed per amp = −17.9,−11.7,−4.3,−2.3,−8.0,**+11.5**,−2.2,−19.0,−6.8 (rho=+0.20 p=0.61); sign-flips at 3.2V; weakest stim (0.5V) shows LARGEST bleed. Per-amp bleed is noise-dominated.
  - **GATE VERDICT: PARTIAL** — per-amp `gain(amp)·profile(d)` model NOT supported (gain unreliable). A single POOLED, amplitude-INDEPENDENT bleed pattern IS supported. Exported cp_bleed_spatial_decay.png.
**Why:** Stage 1 is the GATE for the spatial-decay residual route (residual=actual−clean-contra, replacing err4 per JOURNAL 2026-06-17). Validate the bleed model before retiring err4. Fixed a dim bug (uAmp_cp is [10×1] col; corr transpose mismatch).
**Next:** Stage 2 → POOLED amplitude-independent bleed removal (α=1, no per-amp gain), likely in `contra_residual.m`. OPEN: why is contra bleed amplitude-independent (stim artifact vs onset-locked arousal/hemodynamic)? — affects residual interpretation. Then residual DV (0–200ms inhibition energy) + amp/state characterization.

### 2026-06-17 — Option C shifted-window in build_onset_artifact (corrected)
**Changed/Found:** `utils/build_onset_artifact.m` — implemented the actual Option C: shifted_window mode computes a matched spontaneous reference window shifted `spont_offset_f` frames (~20 s default) before each stim onset, averages across trials, and subtracts from the stim-locked average. Result = stim_mean − spont_mean = pure stim-specific artifact. 'detrend' and 'none' modes retained. `contra_prediction.m` — default `bleed_spont_ref = 'shifted_window'`, `bleed_spont_offset_s = 20`; both call sites guard for missing workspace vars and pass `round(bleed_spont_offset_s * Fs)` as the frame offset. Previous 'detrend' commit was a wrong substitution — this corrects it.
**Why:** User pointed out the shifted-window subtraction (artifact = stim_mean − spont_mean) was agreed but not implemented — linear detrend was substituted without disclosure.
**Next:** Re-run CP-IMP and CP-4; check console for spont-window validity warnings (if many trials report fewer valid spont frames than stim frames, the 20 s offset is too large for the session's recording length — reduce bleed_spont_offset_s).

### 2026-06-17 — Option C detrend in build_onset_artifact (bleed_spont_ref)
**Changed/Found:** `utils/build_onset_artifact.m` — added optional `spont_ref` arg ('none' | 'detrend'). 'detrend' fits a line to each mode's pre-stim window per trial and extrapolates it forward; subtracts this expected spontaneous trajectory instead of the flat pre-stim mean. Isolates the stim-specific deflection, removing slow hemodynamic/arousal drifts correlated with trial timing. `contra_prediction.m` — added `bleed_spont_ref = 'detrend'` knob in Setup section; threaded through both build_onset_artifact call sites (CP-IMP and CP-4).
**Why:** Current mean-subtraction baseline leaves any pre-stim slope extrapolated into the stim window, inflating the artifact estimate and degrading decontamination. Linear detrend removes this without needing a fixed-offset spontaneous reference window (which risks landing on a prior stim period given unknown ITI).
**Next:** Re-run CP-IMP and CP-4 sections; compare art_ratio4 and held-out R² before/after. If decontam quality improves (lower art_ratio, higher R²), keep 'detrend' as default. If no change, the drifts weren't the limiting factor — investigate pixel-space bleed detection instead.

User queried whether the bleed detection gates on dip-vs-baseline-variance; it does not — it uses the signed trial-averaged mean change as a continuous weight.
**Why:** User wanted to understand the detection criterion and verify whether it matches their mental model (SNR-style gating). It doesn't — current method is soft weighting. Note added explaining how to add SNR threshold if desired.
**Next:** Decide whether to add hard SNR gating (require dip > k × baseline SD before pixel projection) or leave as soft weighting.

### 2026-06-17 — CP-6c/6d state splits + dip/recovery dissociation finalized (decision: report split)
**Changed/Found:** `contra_prediction.m` — added CP-6c (pre-stim variance split) + CP-6d (pre-stim delta split), median hi/lo on motion-excluded trials, mirroring CP-6b (trace means + error-bar + t-test); rewrote CP-6w to report DISJOINT dip (0-200ms) vs recovery (200-500ms) vs full (0-500ms) windows. RAN. Results (motexcl n=561, canonical err = full 0-500ms):
  - **Dissociation (CP-6w):** dip 0-200ms → variance p=0.25, delta p=0.16, motion p=0.86 (ALL NULL). recovery 200-500ms → **variance r=+0.130 p=0.002 (Spearman p<1e-4), delta r=+0.114 p=0.007 (Spearman p<1e-4)**, motion p=0.61 (null). Recovery effect is STRONGER than full window. State effect is exclusively in the recovery, absent in the inhibition dip.
  - **CP-6c variance split:** High=0.703±0.033 vs Low=0.567±0.026, **t-test p=0.0014** (high pre-stim variance → less predictable).
  - **CP-6d delta split:** High=0.685±0.029 vs Low=0.585±0.031, **t-test p=0.019** (high pre-stim delta → less predictable).
  - **CP-6b motion split:** High=0.611 vs Low=0.625, no difference (motion null, as expected).
  New PNGs: cp6c_pvar_split.png, cp6d_delta_split.png.
**Why:** User chose "keep both windows, report the dip/recovery split" + "add CP-6c/6d". Confirms the refined claim: inhibition DEPTH is state-invariant; post-inhibition RECOVERY predictability is state-dependent (pre-stim synchronization: variance+delta), NOT motion.
**Next:** Fill FINDINGS.md (variance r=+0.119 p=0.005 / delta r=+0.108 p=0.011 full; recovery-window + split t-tests; motion clean null); draft results.tex pre-stim-brain-state subsection around the dissociation. Commit + push.

### 2026-06-17 — Motion-free baseline + window matters: state effect lives in recovery, not dip
**Changed/Found:** `contra_prediction.m` — dropped motion from the contra OLS baseline (`X_cp_m = X_cp_z`, RRR `cols_r` de-motioned) to remove the motion-as-regressor-AND-split circularity; added [CP-6w] diagnostic that recomputes cp_err over 0-200ms vs 0-500ms and re-runs state correlations. RAN (warm workspace, selExp=3 AL_0033). Result: held-out R²=0.899 (was 0.898 w/ motion → motion adds ~nothing to prediction). State correlations (motexcl n=561):
  - **0-200ms (inhibition dip):** Motion r=-0.006 p=0.86; PreVar r=+0.049 p=0.25; PreDelta r=+0.059 p=0.16 — **ALL NULL**.
  - **0-500ms (dip+recovery):** Motion r=+0.019 p=0.60 (null); **PreVar r=+0.119 p=0.005 (Spearman rho=+0.171 p<1e-4)**; **PreDelta r=+0.108 p=0.011 (Spearman rho=+0.158 p=2e-4)** — variance & delta SIG, motion null.
  - CP-6b motion split (z>0): High=0.611±0.045 vs Low=0.625±0.020 — no difference.
**Why:** User asked to test whether stim quality is state-dependent via the contra+stim route; needed motion non-circular and both candidate error windows compared before picking canonical. Removing motion from baseline also STRENGTHENED the variance correlation (was "ns" in CP-4e when motion was in baseline → now p=0.005), confirming motion was confounding it.
**Next:** USER DECISION pending on canonical error window. Scientific fork: 0-200ms = inhibition depth is state-INDEPENDENT (clean null); 0-500ms = post-inhibition recovery predictability IS state-dependent (variance+delta, NOT motion). Once chosen, set err_end_s, re-export, update FINDINGS.md/manuscript. Premise correction: motion does NOT make stim more predictable (clean null both windows); the real state variables are pre-stim variance + delta, and only in the recovery window.

### 2026-06-17 — State-effect audit of contra+stim pipeline: 2 bugs found (parallel-agent debug)
**Found:** `contra_prediction.m` — (1) **Motion circularity**: motion is a regressor in the contra OLS baseline (`X_cp_m = [X_cp_z, mot_z]`, ~line 236) AND the CP-6b split / CP-6 correlation variable (~lines 1351, 1413). So `cp_err` is a motion-regressed residual; splitting/correlating it by motion is partially circular — likely explains why motion looks NULL on `cperr` (r=-0.015) while it's significant on the original `peakdev` metric (z>1.5, p=0.017). Motion also gets a different trial set (no motExcl) than pvar/delta (motExcl4), so it's not directly comparable. (2) **Stale window/alpha labels**: `err_end_s=0.5`/`cp4_alpha=0.9` (lines 874/877), but commit 37108fc had set 0.2/1.0; revert in 83f8f55 was undocumented. Line 1017 comment still says "alpha=1", line 1356 fprintf still says "MSE 0-200ms" (actual = 0-500ms, per lines 1036/1480). cp_err exported to downstream scripts is therefore 0-500ms MSE, not the 0-200ms inhibition-energy window the original finding was built on.
**Why:** User wants to condition contra+stim prediction error on motion/variance/delta state to test whether stim quality itself is state-dependent. Verified by reading current file: err_end_s=0.5, cp4_alpha=0.9 confirmed at lines 874/877; stale labels confirmed at 1017/1356.
**Next:** Decide canonical error window (0.2 vs 0.5 s) and fix stale labels; add a motion-free baseline variant (`beta_cp_nomot`) so the motion-state split is non-circular; add CP-6c (variance split) and CP-6d (delta split) to mirror CP-6b. Reconcile premise: pipeline records motion-NULL/delta-SIG on cperr, opposite to user's stated "high motion more predictable" framing.

### 2026-06-16 — Motion effect is a THRESHOLD (z>1.5) effect; split motion/prestim into two parallel streams
**Found:** Built high-motion example figure `paper/images/figure2/cp_highmotion_examples.png` (12 highest-motion trials, actual vs contra-pred vs ARX+TF) after the user flagged the "motion doesn't matter" result as contrary to the original setup. Several high-motion trials ARE badly predicted (e.g. 2.7V motZ=4.2 err=2.00). Threshold sweep on CP-4 error (selExp=3): z>0 p=0.78 ns, z>0.5 p=0.50, z>1.0 p=0.27, **z>1.5 p=0.017 SIG (Hi med 0.66 vs Lo 0.50), z>2.0 p=0.020**; Spearman all-trials rho=-0.01 ns, Pearson r=+0.075. => Motion corrupts prediction ONLY above gross-motion z>1.5 (= project motThresh=1.5); the linear/rank correlation and the z>0 CP-6b split are null because mild motion (the bulk) genuinely doesn't matter. RECONCILES old-vs-new: original setup used the 1.5 threshold (where motion matters); CP-6b used z>0 (where it doesn't). Nothing broken — effect is real but nonlinear/threshold.
**Changed:** `motion_analysis.m` + `prestim_variance.m` — added `dev_metric` switch ('peakdev' = original |Peak_imp-mean| deviation-from-trial-avg | 'cperr' = CP-4 contra-pred error). Selection, y-labels, and EXPORT FILENAMES all tagged with the stream so the two never overwrite. `run_all.m` loops both streams. Added `expColors` guard so motion_analysis runs standalone. Verified both streams run clean and produce `..._peakdev` / `..._cperr` outputs; pooled motion r: peakdev -0.131, cperr -0.075.
**Why:** User: "keep two separate streams — original (motion/variance vs per-trial deviation from trial average) AND the contra-prediction route." Plus wanted to see high-motion example predictions.
**Next:** Decide canonical motion threshold (1.5) + error window for the paper. prestim `prevar_heatmap_with_blockfit.pdf` (fig_pvs 2H) is not yet stream-tagged (uses its own pred-error metric). Stale untagged orphans `imp_motion_devscatter_all_sessions.pdf` / `prevar_vs_dev_allamps_motexcl_AL_0033...pdf` can be deleted.
